The listing is CSV with header `value,count`, rows ordered by count descending and then value ascending by byte value, using trimmed non-null values only.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The column name is matched exactly and case-sensitively against the trimmed header.\n- A column name absent from the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output and one diagnostic line on standard error.\n- Combinations that are usage errors (`--column` with `--freq`, `--top` without `--freq`, a non-positive `--top`) are rejected with exit 2 by the usage-errors story before this dispatch runs.\n- Output is built in memory and written to standard output only on success, so a failing command writes nothing to standard output.\n- A column with no non-null values writes only the header.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC freq-default-matches-fixture ===\nIntent: The default frequency listing for a named column equals the top five counts computed independently from the fixture.\nRequires: executable=go; Consumes    | freq.Count, freq.Top, cli.ParseArgs |\n\n## Questions\n\n- None.\n\n## Purpose\n\nWhen `--freq <name>` is given, `cli.Run` writes the frequency listing for that column and never the profile. `--top <n>` limits the rows and defaults to 5. The listing is CSV with header `value,count`, rows ordered by count descending and then value ascending by byte value, using trimmed non-null values only.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The column name is matched exactly and case-sensitively against the trimmed header.\n- A column name absent from the header exits 3 with nothing on standard output and one diagnostic line on standard error.\n- Combinations that are usage errors (`--column` with `--freq`, `--top` without `--freq`, a non-positive `--top`) are rejected with exit 2 by the usage-errors story before this dispatch runs.\n- Output is built in memory and written to standard output only on success, so a failing command writes nothing to standard output.\n- A column with no non-null values writes only the header.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC freq-default-matches-fixture ===\nIntent: The default frequency listing for a named column equals the top five counts computed independently from the fixture.\nRequires: executable=go; Rejected input exits 3. The diagnostic names the file and, for a rejected record, its line number. Standard error is never compared by the grader, so its wording is free; its shape (one line, nothing on standard output) is the contract.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- Rejected input (empty file, file containing only blank lines, empty header name, repeated header name, ragged record) exits 3.\n- Standard output is written only after the whole result is built, so no partial output precedes a failure.\n- Exactly one non-blank line is written to standard error per failure.\n- A ragged record's diagnostic includes the line number of the offending record.\n- Exit code 1 (unreadable file) and exit code 2 (usage) share the same stdout-empty, one-line-stderr rule and are owned by their own stories.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC diag-ragged-fixture ===\nIntent: The supplied ragged fixture exits 3, writes nothing to standard output, and writes one diagnostic line.\nRequires: executable=go; It is used when the file named on the command line does not exist, is not permitted to be read, or fails while being read. It is distinct from exit 3, which means the file was read but its content was rejected.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- An open or read error from the operating system maps to exit 1, not exit 3.\n- Standard output stays empty.\n- One diagnostic line naming the file is written to standard error.\n- This applies to every invocation form: profile, `--column`, and `--freq`.\n- Usage errors (exit 2) take precedence over file access, because argument parsing completes before the file is opened.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC unreadable-missing-file ===\nIntent: A path that does not exist exits 1 and writes nothing to standard output.\nRequires: executable=go; It is a POSIX `sh` script that changes to the application root, runs `go vet ./...`, then `go test ./...`, and exits non-zero if either fails. The project suite, written beside the code, covers each type, each statistic, null handling, rounding, each output form, and each exit code, using files the tests write to temporary directories.\n\n## Behavior\n\n- The script uses only POSIX `sh` constructs and resolves the application root from its own location, so it works from any current directory.\n- It runs `go vet ./...` first and stops with a non-zero status if vet fails.\n- It then runs `go test ./...` and exits with that command's status.\n- It never modifies files under `sources/`.\n- The suite covers:\n  - every column type (integer, number, date, boolean, text, empty);\n  - rows, nulls, distinct, min, max, sum, mean, and median;\n  - null spellings and trimming;\n  - half-away-from-zero rounding and no negative zero;\n  - the profile form, the `--column` form, and the `--freq` form;\n  - exit codes 0, 1, 2, and 3.\n\n## Programmatic Acceptance\n\n=== AC testgate-script-exits-zero ===\nIntent: Running bin/test.sh from the application root exits zero.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\n\nresult = subprocess.run([\"sh\", \"bin/test.sh\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0, result.returncode\n=== END AC testgate-script-exits-zero ===\n\n=== AC testgate-runs-from-other-directory ===\nIntent: bin/test.sh locates the application root itself and exits zero when started from another directory.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport os\nimport subprocess\nimport tempfile\n\nscript = os.path.abspath(\"bin/test.sh\")\nwith tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:\n    result = subprocess.run([\"sh\", script], capture_output=True, text=True, cwd=tmp)\nprint(result.stdout)\nprint(result.stderr)\nassert result.returncode == 0, result.returncode\n=== END AC testgate-runs-from-other-directory ===\n\n=== AC testgate-go-test-and-vet-pass ===\nIntent: go vet and go test pass directly at the application root, the two commands the script wraps.\nRequires: executable=go; scope=test\n\nimport subprocess\n\nvet = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"vet\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(vet.stdout)\nprint(vet.stderr)\nassert vet.returncode == 0, vet.returncode\n\ntests = subprocess.run([\"go\", \"test\", \"./...\"], capture_output=True, text=True)\nprint(tests.stdout)\nprint(tests.stderr)\nassert tests.returncode == 0, tests.returncode\n=== END AC testgate-go-test-and-vet-pass ===\n\n=== AC testgate-suite-has-tests ===\nIntent: The project ships Go test files in more than one internal package, so the gate is not vacuous.\nRequires: executable=go;
